## Analysis Summary

### Comparison Snapshot

| Metric | Value |
|---|---|
| Runs covered | `90` |
| Scored events | `70` |
| Excluded `non-AV` events | `113` |
| Excluded `accidental` events | `204` |
| Overall success rate | `91.4% (64/70)` |
| `unpudo` success rate | `90.3% (56/62)` |
| `unparking` success rate | `100.0% (8/8)` |
| Ownership / handover failures | `50.0% (3/6)` |
| Short AV-attempt failures | `50.0% (3/6)` |
| Successful events with no driver accel help | `100.0% (64/64)` |
| Successful events with route change found | `95.3% (61/64)` |
| Successful gear alignment at anchor | `100.0% (64/64)` |

### Failure Profile

- The scored subset contains `6` failures out of `70` scored events. The dominant effective failure types are `failed_to_unpudo` 33.3% (2/6), `driver_completed_maneuver` 33.3% (2/6), `failed_to_slow` 16.7% (1/6).
- Ownership loss and handover breakage are the main theme: `3` of `6` failures fall into `not_av_owned`, `completed_outside_av`, `driver_completed_maneuver`, or `interrupted_handover`.
- Route-change evidence inside failed events is `found` in `6`, `not found` in `0`, and `unclear` in `0` cases.
- Source disengagement labels inside failed events are `failed_to_unpudo` 33.3% (2/6), `failed_to_slow` 16.7% (1/6); anything else is failing from DBW / ownership / gear evidence rather than an explicit source disengagement label.
- Event-type split: `unparking` is stronger than `unpudo` in this sample: 100.0% (8/8) versus 90.3% (56/62).

### Excluded Events

- `113` events are labelled `non-AV`: the detected maneuver starts outside AV ownership, so they are mentioned for coverage but excluded from model scoring.
- `204` events are labelled `accidental`: AV participates for less than `2s`, so they are treated as accidental contact rather than a meaningful model attempt.

### Success Behavior

- Successful events are distributed as `56` `unpudo` and `8` `unparking`.
- On successful attempts, route change is recovered in `61` of `64` cases and driver accelerator help is absent in `64` of `64` cases.
- Gear state stays aligned at the anchor in `64` of `64` comparable passes. Planned-indicator alignment is `26.6% (17/64)`, and controller-indicator alignment is `26.6% (17/64)`.
- Typical successful timing is median `AV -> event start` `8.63s`, median `AV -> first motion` `8.67s`, and median AV-owned duration `134.78s`.
- Typical successful maneuver shape: common actual gear at the anchor is `DRIVE_POSITION_V2_DRIVE`, common actual indicator is `INDICATORS_STATE_V2_OFF`, and median max AV speed is `4.99 m/s`.

### Written Assessment

This card covers `387` detected events across `90` runs, but only `70` of them are scored AV-owned attempts. The remaining `113` `non-AV` and `204` `accidental` events are explicitly excluded from the success-rate denominator. Within the scored subset, the overall success rate is `91.4% (64/70)`. `unparking` is stronger than `unpudo` in this sample: 100.0% (8/8) versus 90.3% (56/62).

Across the scored failures, route-change evidence is `6` found / `0` not found / `0` unclear, so navigation context is usually present. The failure mix is led by `failed_to_unpudo` 33.3% (2/6), `driver_completed_maneuver` 33.3% (2/6), `failed_to_slow` 16.7% (1/6), while the excluded portion is mostly `non-AV` starts or sub-`2s` accidental AV contact rather than true scored failures.

When this model succeeds, the behavior is consistent: route change is recovered in `61` of `64` successful events, driver accelerator help is absent in `64` of `64`, and gear alignment holds in `64` of `64` comparable passes. The typical successful event starts moving about `8.67s` after AV engagement, reaches a median max AV speed of `4.99 m/s`, and most often begins in gear `DRIVE_POSITION_V2_DRIVE` with indicator state `INDICATORS_STATE_V2_OFF`.
